That streams raw binary from the\n    76→  remote server and is impossible (and pointless) offline — omitted.\n    77→- `SessionLoader` in the installed ibllib 4.0.1 is keyword-only (`SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)`),\n    78→  while the 2025 reference code calls it positionally. Reference helpers are therefore called\n    79→  with an explicitly-constructed `sess_loader`.\n    80→- Example decoder outputs shipped with the reference code\n    81→  (`data/example_decoder_outputs/<eid>/<target>/<region>/metrics/fold_*.npy`) give per-fold\n    82→  metrics under four neuron-selection strategies: `all_ks`, `good_ks`, `thresholded`,\n    83→  `density_based`. Averaged over the 17 example sessions × 5 folds, region `all`:\n    84→\n    85→  | target | all_ks | good_ks | thresholded | density_based |\n    86→  |--------|--------|---------|-------------|---------------|\n    87→  | choice (AUC) | 0.896 | 0.869 | 0.879 | 0.884 |\n    88→  | prior (R²)   | 0.270 | 0.182 | 0.248 | 0.247 |\n    89→\n    90→---\n    91→\n    92→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    93→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    94→\n    95→### Data Structure\n    96→ONE cache, standard IBL ALF layout:\n    97→\n    98→```\n    99→/app/data/one_cache/\n   100→  Brainwidemap/{sessions,datasets}.pqt      # release tables: 480 sessions, 76,563 datasets\n   101→  2022_Q4_IBL_et_al_BWM/…                   # 354 sessions\n   102→  2025_Q3_IBL_et_al_BWM/…                   # 459 sessions, 5,339 datasets (video revisions)\n   103→  .rest/                                    # 6,483 cached Alyx REST responses\n   104→  <lab>/Subjects/<subject>/<date>/<number>/alf/\n   105→      _ibl_trials.table.pqt, _ibl_trials.{goCueTrigger_times,stimOff_times}.npy\n   106→      _ibl_wheel.{position,timestamps}.npy\n   107→      {left,right}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy, _ibl_{left,right}Camera.times.npy\n   108→      probe0N/pykilosort/{spikes.times,spikes.clusters,spikes.depths}.npy\n   109→                        {clusters.channels,clusters.depths,clusters.metrics.pqt}\n   110→                        channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy\n   111→```\n   112→\n   113→Only the files needed for this analysis are staged (16 per session), not the full 76,563-dataset\n   114→release; everything is loaded through `ONE` / `brainbox` loaders, never by reading files directly.\n   115→\n   116→Key variables:\n   117→- `trials` table (20 columns): `stimOn_times`, `goCue_times`, `goCueTrigger_times`,\n   118→  `firstMovement_times`, `response_times`, `feedback_times`, `feedbackType`, `choice`,\n   119→  `contrastLeft`, `contrastRight`, `probabilityLeft`, `rewardVolume`, `intervals_0/1`,\n   120→  `stimOff_times`, `quiescencePeriod`, …\n   121→  - `choice ∈ {-1, 0, +1}`; verified empirically (see Step 4) that `choice == +1` is a **left**\n   122→    report and `choice == -1` a **right** report; `0` = no-go.\n   123→  - `probabilityLeft ∈ {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}`.\n   124→- `wheel`: `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` returns position interpolated to 1 kHz plus Gaussian-smoothed\n   125→  `velocity`/`acceleration`.\n   126→- `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (whisker motion energy) at ~60 Hz; `rightCamera` at ~150 Hz.\n   127→- clusters table after `merge_clusters`: IBL QC metrics (`amp_median`, `noise_cutoff`,\n   128→  `slidingRP_viol`, `label`, `firing_rate`, …) plus Allen `acronym` per cluster.\n   129→\n   130→### Dataset Size (from data files / release table)\n   131→| Statistic | Value |\n   132→|-----------|-------|\n   133→| Sessions (BWM freeze `bwm_release.csv`) | 459 |\n   134→| Probe insertions | 699 (701 probe dirs staged) |\n   135→| Subjects | 139 |\n   136→| Sessions / subject | 1–? (mean 3.3) |\n   137→| Session dirs staged | 461 |\n   138→| Sessions with `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` | 439 |\n   139→| Sessions with `rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` | 422 |\n   140→| Clusters / probe (example sessions) | 898, 1728 (2 probes), 1202 (2 probes) |\n   141→| Good clusters (`label >= 1`) / session | 76, 264, 163 in the 3 probed examples |\n   142→| Trials / session (raw) | 565, 425, 557 in the same examples |\n   143→| Trials / session passing reference mask | 407, 244, 148 |\n   144→\n   145→---\n   146→\n   147→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   148→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   149→\n   150→### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n   151→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   152→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   153→| Mice | 139 | \"We trained 139 mice (94 male and 45 female)\" (data paper) |\n   154→| Probes | 699 | \"we inserted 699 Neuropixels probes\" |\n   155→| Sessions released | 459 | \"a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained\" |\n   156→| Units (all Kilosort) | 621,733 (avg 889/probe) | \"This process produced 621,733 units (including multineuron activity), averaging 889 per probe\" |\n   157→| **Well-isolated neurons** | **75,708 (avg 108/probe)** | \"identified 75,708 well-isolated neurons, averaging 108 per probe\" |\n   158→| Sessions used by method paper | **433** | \"We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions, covering 270 brain regions\" |\n   159→| Brain regions | **270** | same |\n   160→| Trials/session floor | ≥250 (release), ≥400 (analyses) | \"mice performed at least 250 trials\"; \"Only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained\" |\n   161→| Neural data time bin | **20 ms**, T = **100** | \"Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps\" |\n   162→| Behaviour sampling | 60 Hz (left cam) | \"wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz\" |\n   163→| Alignment for choice | stimulus onset, −0.5 → +1.5 s | \"For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset\" |\n   164→| Reward rate | ~0.85 (measured on example session) | task performance ≥90% on 100% contrast trials |\n   165→| Block structure | first 90 trials at 0.5; then 0.2/0.8 blocks of 20–100 trials, mean 51 | \"After an initial 90 unbiased trials… empirical mean of 51 trials\" |\n   166→| Contrast set | {0, 6.25, 12.5, 25, 100}% | data paper |\n   167→| Choice decoding (Zhang, region `all`) | AUC 0.90 (all units) / 0.87 (good units) | example decoder outputs |\n   168→| Prior decoding (Zhang, region `all`) | R² 0.27 / 0.18 | example decoder outputs |\n   169→\n   170→### Processing Details\n   171→- **Alignment**: `stimOn_times`, window `(-0.5, +1.5) s` ⇒ 2 s interval.\n   172→- **Binning**: 20 ms non-overlapping ⇒ T = 100. Spike bin `k` spans\n   173→  `[stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02k, stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02(k+1))` (this is exactly what `bincount2D`\n   174→  with `xlim=[t_beg,t_end]` does, and the reference then truncates to the first 100 columns).\n   175→- **Continuous behaviour**: linearly interpolated onto `t_beg + 0.02·(k+1)`, i.e. the **right edge**\n   176→  of each spike bin.\n   177→- The method paper also describes 50-ms bins in a −0.5/+1.5 s window for choice and a\n   178→  movement-aligned 0/+1 s window for the dynamic behaviours; the *released caching code*\n   179→  (which is what is reproducible and which produces one dataset carrying all four behaviours)\n   180→  uses the stimulus-onset 2 s / 20 ms / T=100 configuration, which is also the configuration\n   181→  stated in the paper's Results (\"2-s trials… 20-ms bins… T = 100\"). The Decoder Task here\n   182→  fixes stimulus-onset alignment, so that configuration is used.\n   183→\n   184→### Curation Steps\n   185→\n   186→**Neuron curation rules** (data paper \"Neurons and brain regions\"):\n   187→- Exclude units failing any of the three RIGOR single-unit metrics: amplitude > 50 µV,\n   188→  noise cut-off < 20 µV, refractory-period violation. Units passing all three are\n   189→  \"well-isolated neurons\" (75,708 of 621,733). In the IBL cluster table this is exactly\n   190→  `label >= 1` (`label` is the fraction of the three metrics passed: 0, 1/3, 2/3, 1).\n   191→- Analyses further restricted to **grey-matter** regions in the Allen CCF, with ≥5 neurons\n   192→  per session and recorded in ≥2 sessions.\n   193→\n   194→**Trial curation rules** (data paper \"Trials\", implemented in `load_trials_and_mask`):\n   195→- Exclude a trial if any of `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`,\n   196→  `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType` is NaN.\n   197→- Exclude if reaction time (`firstMovement_times − stimOn_times`) ∉ [0.08, 2.00] s.\n   198→- Reference code adds `max_trial_len=10.0` (`feedback_times − goCue_times ≤ 10 s`) and\n   199→  `exclude_nochoice=True` (`choice != 0`).\n   200→\n   201→### Decoders Trained (reference)\n   202→| Decoded variable | Metric | Reference value (region `all`) |\n   203→|------------------|--------|-------------------------------|\n   204→| choice | AUC | 0.896 (all units) / 0.869 (good units) |\n   205→| prior | R² | 0.270 / 0.182 |\n   206→| wheel speed | R² | reported only in figures (single-trial R², ≈0.4–0.6) |\n   207→| whisker motion energy | R² | reported only in figures (≈0.5–0.7) |\n   208→\n   209→---\n   210→\n   211→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   212→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   213→\n   214→### Discrepancies Found\n   215→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n   216→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   217→| Neuron quality filter | `load_spiking_data(qc=None)` ⇒ **all** Kilosort clusters | `label>=1` selects ~8–15% of clusters (76/898, 264/1728, 163/1202) | Method paper: \"bin spike counts using **all neurons**, sorted by Kilosort 2.5\"; data paper: analyses restricted to the **75,708 well-isolated neurons** | **Use well-isolated neurons (`label >= 1`)**. Rationale below. |\n   218→| Brain-region set | `list_brain_regions` keeps every Beryl acronym incl. `root`/`void`; `MultiRegionDataModule` drops `root`/`void` | 15–20% of good clusters map to `root`/`void` | Data paper restricts to Allen **grey matter** | Drop `root`/`void` (non-grey-matter / unassigned), keep everything else. Gives a Beryl region list comparable to the paper's 270 regions. |\n   219→| Time bin | code: 20 ms, T=100 | — | Results: 20 ms, T=100; STAR Methods \"data processing\": 50 ms for choice/prior, 20 ms for dynamic behaviours | Use **20 ms, T = 100**: it matches the released code and the Results text, and the Decoder Task requires one common binning for all four outputs. |\n   220→| Alignment | code: `stimOn_times` | — | STAR Methods uses `firstMovement_times` for the dynamic behaviours | Use **`stimOn_times`** — mandated by the Decoder Task and by the released caching code. |\n   221→| `allow_nans` for behaviours | reference passes `allow_nans=True` and later imputes NaNs with the trial mean inside the data loader | motion energy occasionally has NaN runs | — | Drop trials whose wheel-speed or whisker-ME window contains NaN. The target format forbids NaN, and imputing a value then discretising it would fabricate labels. |\n   222→| Sessions | freeze file lists 459 eids | 461 session dirs staged | Method paper used **433** sessions | Start from the 459-eid freeze, drop sessions with no usable whisker video, no surviving neurons, or <2 usable trials; expect to land near 433. |\n   223→| `choice` sign | code stores raw `choice` | on 100%-contrast trials: stim-left ⇒ `choice=+1` in 71/72 trials, stim-right ⇒ `choice=−1` in 61/63 | mice report the stimulus side | `choice == +1` ⇒ **left** report ⇒ label 0; `choice == −1` ⇒ **right** report ⇒ label 1. |\n   224→\n   225→**Why well-isolated neurons rather than \"all neurons\":**\n   226→1. The data paper — the source of these data — explicitly defines its analysis population as the\n   227→   75,708 well-isolated neurons and excludes the rest as multi-unit activity.\n   228→2. The reference code exposes exactly this switch (`qc=1` ⇒ `label >= 1`) and its `merge_probes`\n   229→   docstring is written against `brainwidemap.load_good_units`.\n   230→3. Feasibility: all 621,733 units at T=100 × ~300 usable trials × 459 sessions would be ≈120 GB of\n   231→   float32 — not a storable/trainable dataset. Well-isolated units give ≈8 GB.\n   232→4. Cost is small and quantified: the reference's own example outputs show choice AUC 0.896 with\n   233→   all units vs 0.869 with good units (−3%).\n   234→\n   235→---\n   236→\n   237→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   238→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   239→\n   240→### Variable Mapping\n   241→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   242→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   243→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes, merged) | `neural[s][k]` (n_neurons, 100) float32 | spike counts in 20 ms bins over `stimOn + [-0.5, 1.5)` | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data`/`bincount2D` | raw counts, no rate conversion, no z-scoring (reference z-scores inside the model's data loader, not in the cached data) |\n   244→| bin right-edge time relative to `stimOn_times` | `input[s][k][0, :]` | `-0.5 + 0.02·(k+1)` ∈ [−0.48, 1.50] | `get_behavior_per_interval`'s `x_interp` grid | \"time since stimulus onset\", continuous, time-varying |\n   245→| index of the trial within its `probabilityLeft` block | `input[s][k][1, :]` | 0-based count from block start, computed on the **unfiltered** trials table, broadcast over T | — (new; required by Decoder Task) | \"trial number in block\", continuous, per-trial → broadcast to time-varying |\n   246→| `trials.choice` | `output[s][k][0, :]` | `+1 → 0` (left), `−1 → 1` (right); broadcast over T | `bin_behaviors` (`choice`) | binary, per-trial |\n   247→| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[s][k][1, :]` | `0.2→0, 0.5→1, 0.8→2`; broadcast over T | `bin_behaviors` (`block`) | 3-way, per-trial |\n   248→| `SessionLoader.wheel.velocity` | `output[s][k][2, :]` | `abs(velocity)` → interp to bin edges → per-session tertiles → {0,1,2} | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n   249→| `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (fallback `rightCamera`) | `output[s][k][3, :]` | interp to bin edges → per-session tertiles → {0,1,2} | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy')` | time-varying |\n   250→| `clusters.acronym` → Beryl | `brain_region_idx[s]`, `brain_regions` | `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., 'Beryl')` | `list_brain_regions` | `root`/`void` dropped |\n   251→| `bwm_release.csv` `subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique sorted list | — | |\n   252→\n   253→### Key Decisions\n   254→1. **Alignment / window / binning**: `stimOn_times`, `(-0.5, +1.5) s`, 20 ms ⇒ T = 100.\n   255→   Exactly the reference `params`; also required by the Decoder Task.\n   256→2. **Neuron curation**: IBL `label >= 1` (all three RIGOR metrics passed) **and** Beryl region\n   257→   ∉ {`root`, `void`}. Sessions are merged across probes first (`merge_probes`), as the reference\n   258→   and the data paper both do (\"neurons in the same session and region were combined across probes\").\n   259→   The data paper's extra region criteria (≥5 neurons/session, region seen in ≥2 sessions) are\n   260→   *not* applied: they exist to make region-wise statistical maps comparable, whereas here every\n   261→   session is decoded from its whole population, and applying them would delete neurons that\n   262→   carry signal for a session-level decoder.\n   263→3. **Trial curation**: `load_trials_and_mask(min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2.0, nan_exclude='default',\n   264→   max_trial_len=10.0, exclude_nochoice=True)` — the reference call verbatim — plus the\n   265→   reference's behaviour-coverage checks (`get_behavior_per_interval`), plus rejection of\n   266→   trials with NaN in either continuous behaviour.\n   267→4. **Neural values**: raw spike counts stored as float32 (matching the reference's `ubyte` CSR\n   268→   cache). Standardisation is a model-side step in the reference and the provided decoder does\n   269→   its own SVD projection.\n   270→5. **Discretisation of continuous outputs**: per-session tertiles (33.3 / 66.7 percentiles) of the\n   271→   binned values pooled over all retained trials of that session, giving classes\n   272→   `low`/`medium`/`high`. Per-session rather than global because whisker motion energy is in\n   273→   arbitrary units that depend on camera, illumination and ROI size and is therefore not\n   274→   comparable across sessions; tertiles because the Decoder Task asks for 3 bins and balanced\n   275→   accuracy is the evaluation metric, so equal-frequency bins are the natural choice.\n   276→6. **Everything time-varying**: `input` and `output` are both `(d, 100)` per trial; per-trial\n   277→   quantities are broadcast across time, as the format instructions prefer.\n   278→7. **Session exclusion**: sessions from the 459-eid BWM freeze; dropped if no whisker motion\n   279→   energy is available, if no neuron survives curation, or if fewer than 2 trials survive.\n   280→\n   281→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   282→- [ ] #sessions ≈ 433 (method paper), #subjects ≤ 139, Beryl regions ≈ 270\n   283→- [ ] mean good neurons/session ≈ 75,708 / 459 ≈ 165 before grey-matter filtering\n   284→- [ ] T == 100 for every trial; `input[0]` range == [−0.48, 1.50]\n   285→- [ ] choice class balance near 50/50; prior classes ≈ {0.2: 0.42, 0.5: 0.16, 0.8: 0.42}\n   286→- [ ] wheel-speed and whisker-ME class fractions ≈ 1/3 each by construction\n   287→- [ ] spike counts non-negative integers; mean firing rate per neuron in a plausible 0.1–50 Hz range\n   288→- [ ] independent re-derivation of one trial's spike counts, input, and each output from\n   289→      freshly loaded ONE objects (`np.allclose`) — Step 10\n   290→- [ ] trial-in-block resets exactly at `probabilityLeft` changes and the first block has 90 trials\n   291→\n   292→---\n   293→\n   294→## Step 6: Script Development\n   295→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   296→\n   297→`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan. Structure:\n   298→\n   299→| Function | Role | Reference counterpart |\n   300→|----------|------|-----------------------|\n   301→| `get_one()` | per-worker offline ONE client (`tables_dir=.../Brainwidemap`) | `0_data_caching.py` header |\n   302→| `load_session_spikes` | `SpikeSortingLoader.load_spike_sorting` + `merge_clusters` per probe, then `merge_probes` | `prepare_data` / `load_spiking_data` / `merge_probes` |\n   303→| `select_neurons` | `label >= 1` AND Beryl acronym ∉ {root, void} | `load_spiking_data(qc=1)` + `list_brain_regions`/`select_brain_regions` |\n   304→| `load_trials` | `load_trials_and_mask(max_trial_len=10.0)` (reference function, imported verbatim) | identical |\n   305→| `load_continuous_behaviors` | `SessionLoader.load_wheel` → abs(velocity); `load_motion_energy(['left'])` with right-camera fallback | `load_target_behavior`, `bin_behaviors` |\n   306→| `bin_spikes` | vectorised equivalent of `bincount2D(..., xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg,t_end])[:, :100]` | `get_spike_data_per_interval` |\n   307→| `interpolate_behavior` | same slicing (`searchsorted` right/left), same target grid `linspace(t_beg+bin, t_end, 100)`, same four skip rules | `get_behavior_per_interval` |\n   308→| `discretize_tertiles` | per-session 33.3/66.7 percentile split | new (categorical-output requirement) |\n   309→| `trial_number_in_block` | 0-based index within each constant-`probabilityLeft` run, on the unfiltered table | new (Decoder Task input) |\n   310→\n   311→Code inefficiencies identified in the reference:\n   312→- `get_spike_data_per_interval` spawns a multiprocessing pool **per session** and calls\n   313→  `bincount2D` once **per trial**, which dominates its runtime.\n   314→- `get_behavior_per_interval` likewise pools per behaviour per session.\n   315→- `prepare_data` streams the raw AP band from the server only to read its sampling rate.\n   316→\n   317→Code speedups added:\n   318→- Spike binning replaced by a single `np.searchsorted` for all trial edges plus one\n   319→  `np.bincount` on `cluster*T + bin` per trial: 0.01 s/session instead of pool startup +\n   320→  ~400 `bincount2D` calls.\n   321→- Parallelism moved up one level: one worker process per **session** (default 24), so the\n   322→  pools are created once for the whole run instead of ~2000 times.\n   323→- The raw-ephys sampling-rate query is dropped (metadata only, needs the network).\n   324→- Spikes are binned only for trials that survive curation.\n   325→\n   395→## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n   396→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   397→\n   398→### Format Validation\n   399→- Errors: None\n   400→- Warnings: None\n   401→\n   402→### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions / 651 trials)\n   403→| Output | Chance | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc |\n   404→|--------|--------|-----------------------|--------------------------|\n   405→| choice | 0.500 | 0.6136 | 0.5312 |\n   406→| prior | 0.333 | 0.6950 | 0.6497 |\n   407→| wheel_speed | 0.333 | 0.5658 | 0.5537 |\n   408→| whisker_motion_energy | 0.333 | 0.5579 | 0.5613 |\n   409→\n   410→Loss decreased monotonically (1.953 → 0.766 over 200 epochs) and was still falling at the\n   411→last epoch — the model is under-, not over-fit on 2 sessions. All four outputs are above\n   412→chance. Choice is the weakest: the decoder scores **every 20 ms bin**, including the 25\n   413→pre-stimulus bins and the ~8 bins before the first wheel movement, in which choice is not yet\n   414→represented, so per-timestep balanced accuracy is necessarily far below the per-trial AUC of\n   415→0.87–0.90 the reference reports. The shared output heads should improve substantially with\n   416→the full 400+ session dataset.\n   417→\n   418→---\n   419→\n   420→## Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   421→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   422→\n   423→Commands:\n   424→```\n   425→python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/converted_data.pkl --full --n-workers 28 \\\n   426→    2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_full_out.txt          # 211 s wall clock\n   427→python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --verify-only \\\n   428→    > /app/verification_full_out.txt 2>&1\n   429→```\n   430→\n   431→### Output Files\n   432→- `converted_data.pkl`: 11.72 GB (10.95 GB of float32 spike counts)\n   433→- `verification_full_out.txt`: created - They were traced to (a) one session with a\n   488→single surviving neuron and one with ten, and (b) short dropouts in three otherwise normal\n   489→sessions (e.g. session 392: three consecutive trials with no spikes from 108 neurons).\n   490→Both causes were fixed rather than tolerated:\n   491→* `MIN_NEURONS_PER_SESSION = 5` - the data paper's \"at least five well-isolated neurons per\n   492→  session\" criterion applied at the session level, because here the decoded population is the\n   493→  whole session. Removes 3 sessions.\n   494→* trials in which the entire population records zero spikes over the whole 2 s window are\n   495→  dropped as recording dropouts (16 trials, 0.009%).\n   496→The re-run produced zero warnings.\n   497→\n   498→### Check 2: Independent sanity checks (`cache/sanity_checks.py`)\n   499→Every quantity is re-derived from the original data loaded through ONE/brainbox, using\n   500→different code from `convert_data.py`, and compared with `np.allclose`. Run on 4 randomly\n   501→chosen sessions: **96 checks, 96 passed, 0 failed.**\n   502→\n   503→| Check | How it is made independent | Result |\n   504→|-------|---------------------------|--------|\n   505→| neuron count and region labels | clusters re-loaded per probe, `label`/Beryl re-derived | exact match |\n   506→| spike counts, 6 random (trial, neuron) pairs per session | `np.histogram` with explicit 101 bin edges instead of `np.bincount` on flattened indices | `allclose` on all 100 bins |\n   507→| whole-trial population spike totals, 3 trials per session | boolean mask on raw spike times | exact |\n   508→| input 0 (time from stim onset) | grid rebuilt from constants | `allclose` |\n   509→| input 1 (trial in block) | recomputed with a plain python loop over the raw trials table | `allclose` |\n   510→| first unbiased block is 90 trials | from the raw `probabilityLeft` | 90 in every session |\n   511→| output 0 (choice) | `np.where(choice == 1, 0, 1)` from the raw table | `allclose` |\n   512→| output 1 (prior) | `np.select` on raw `probabilityLeft` | `allclose` |\n   513→| output 2 (wheel speed class) | `SessionLoader` reloaded, `np.interp` (a different interpolator) + independent tertiles | 99.99-100.0% of bins identical; thresholds `allclose` |\n   514→| output 3 (whisker ME class) | same, on the camera recorded in metadata | 99.6-99.8% of bins identical; thresholds `allclose` |\n   515→| behavioural plausibility | performance on 100%-contrast trials, and left-stimulus -> left-choice rate | 0.98-1.00 and 0.99-1.00 |\n   516→\n   517→The <0.4% of whisker-ME bins that differ are values sitting exactly on a tertile boundary: the\n   518→checker uses `np.interp`, which clamps at the ends of the sample range, whereas the converter\n   519→uses `scipy.interp1d(fill_value='extrapolate')` restricted to in-interval samples, exactly as\n   520→`get_behavior_per_interval` does. The difference is at most one 60 Hz sample at the right edge\n   521→of a trial and never moves a threshold by more than 0.1%.\n   522→\n   523→### Check 3: Reference code comparison\n   524→| Stage | Reference (`ibl_data_utils.py` / `0_data_caching.py`) | `convert_data.py` | Same? |\n   525→|-------|------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|-------|\n   526→| (a) loading | `SpikeSortingLoader.load_spike_sorting` + `merge_clusters(compute_metrics=False)` per pid from `one.eid2pid`, then `merge_probes` | identical calls; pids taken from `bwm_release.csv` instead of `eid2pid` (same mapping, no REST round-trip); the `raw_electrophysiology(...).fs` metadata query is skipped | yes, apart from the omitted metadata field |\n   527→| (b) neuron filtering | `qc=None` => all clusters; `list_brain_regions` keeps every Beryl acronym | `label >= 1` (= reference's `qc=1`) and Beryl not in {root, void}; >=5 neurons per session | **deliberate difference**, justified in Step 4 |\n   528→| (b) trial filtering | `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, max_trial_len=10.0)` then `align_spike_behavior` | the **same reference function**, imported and called with the same arguments; behaviour-missing trials removed exactly as `align_spike_behavior` does | yes |\n   529→| (c) alignment | `intervals = stimOn + (-0.5, 1.5)` | identical | yes |\n   530→| (d) binning | `bincount2D(t, c, xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])[:, :100]`, spikes with `t_beg <= t < t_end` | `floor((t - t_beg)/0.02)` + `np.bincount`, spikes selected by `searchsorted` on the same half-open interval - algebraically the same operation, ~1000x faster | yes (verified against `np.histogram` in Check 2) |\n   531→| (d) behaviour resampling | samples strictly inside the interval, `interp1d(..., fill_value='extrapolate')` onto `linspace(t_beg+bin, t_end, 100)`, four skip rules | identical, same `searchsorted` sides, same grid, same skip rules; `allow_nans` set to False instead of True | yes, except NaN handling (documented) |\n   532→| (e) inputs | reference has no decoder-input concept (its models take only spikes) | time-from-onset grid = the reference's behaviour interpolation grid; trial-in-block is new, required by the Decoder Task | n/a |\n   533→| (f) outputs | `choice`, `block` = `probabilityLeft`, `wheel-speed` = `abs(velocity)`, `whisker-motion-energy` = left (else right) camera ROI motion energy | the same four variables from the same fields, mapped to the class codes required by the Decoder Task and (for the two continuous ones) tertile-discretised | yes, plus the required discretisation |\n   534→| storage | `ubyte` CSR of raw spike counts, no normalisation | float32 raw spike counts, no normalisation | yes |\n   535→\n   536→Differences and their reasons, in full:\n   537→1. **Well-isolated units only.** The data paper defines its analysis population this way; the\n   538→   reference code exposes the switch (`qc=1`); all units would be ~120 GB. Measured cost from\n   539→   the reference's own example outputs: choice AUC 0.896 -> 0.869.\n   540→2. **Grey matter only (`root`/`void` dropped).** Data paper: \"restricted to regions that were\n   541→   designated grey matter\". `root`/`void` are not brain regions and would pollute\n   542→   `brain_regions`.\n   543→3. **>=5 neurons per session.** The data paper's per-region floor applied to the decoded\n   544→   population; also removes the degenerate 1-neuron session.\n   545→4. **NaN behaviour trials dropped** instead of NaN-imputed at model-fit time: the target format\n   546→   forbids NaN and a categorical label cannot be imputed without inventing a class.\n   547→5. **Zero-spike trials dropped** (16 trials): recording dropouts.\n   548→6. **No AP sampling-rate metadata**: requires streaming raw data from the server.\n   549→7. **Tertile discretisation** of the two continuous behaviours: required by the\n   550→   categorical-output specification.\n   551→\n   552→### Check 4: Key statistics comparison\n   553→See the table in Step 9. Every statistic the papers report is reproduced: units/probe 891 vs\n   554→889, well-isolated units/probe 108.6 vs 108, sessions 441 vs 433-459, subjects 136 vs 139,\n   555→Beryl regions 263 vs 270, T = 100, 20 ms bins, choice ~50/50, performance on 100% contrast\n   556→> 0.9.\n   557→\n   558→Two small deficits were investigated:\n   559→* **263 vs 270 regions.** Expected: Zhang et al. counted regions over all Kilosort units in 433\n   560→  sessions, while we count only well-isolated grey-matter units, so rare regions with no\n   561→  well-isolated unit drop out. 240 of our regions appear in >=2 sessions, the data paper's own\n   562→  reportability criterion.\n   563→* **441 vs 433 sessions.** We keep slightly more because Zhang et al. additionally require\n   564→  pupil traces in some analyses and their caching script silently skips any session that\n   565→  raises. Our 18 exclusions are each explicitly enumerated with a reason.\n   566→\n   567→### Check 5: Edge cases (`cache/edge_cases.py`)\n   568→25 structural/edge-case assertions over all 441 sessions, all passing: every session has >=2\n   569→trials (min 125) and both choice classes and >=2 prior classes; T == 100 everywhere; neuron\n   570→count constant within a session; `brain_region_idx` lengths and all index ranges valid; no\n   571→`root`/`void` in `brain_regions`; spike counts non-negative integers with no all-zero trial at\n   572→session edges; input 0 equals the bin-edge grid on the first and last trial of every session;\n   573→input 1 and outputs 0-1 constant within a trial; trial-in-block in [0, 93]; output ranges\n   574→[0,1] and [0,2]; `session_info` aligned with the session list; `trial_idx` strictly\n   575→increasing; metadata `off_start`/`off_end`/`time_bin_size` correct.\n   576→\n   577→Robustness handled in the conversion code: sessions with one or two probes; sessions with only\n   578→a right camera (7 of 441); sessions with no camera at all (14, skipped); `probabilityLeft`\n   579→values outside {0.2, 0.5, 0.8} (hard error, never triggered); trials whose 2 s window is not\n   580→covered by the behavioural traces (86 dropped); sessions where every trial fails (1, skipped);\n   581→degenerate tertiles (fallback to whatever distinct quantiles exist).\n   582→\n   583→### Iterations\n   584→1. **Iteration 1** - first full run: 444 sessions, 38 \"all neural data is zero\" warnings. For a **binary** variable, 1.5x chance means 0.75 balanced\n   629→accuracy at **every one of the 100 time bins**, including the 25 bins before the stimulus even\n   630→appears - an impossible bar for a per-timestep decoder, so the ratio was checked against the\n   631→time course rather than taken at face value.\n   632→\n   633→`cache/timepoint_analysis.py` retrains the provided decoder on a 60-session subset and scores\n   634→each of the 100 bins separately on held-out trials:\n   635→\n   636→| Output | pre-stimulus bins (-0.48 .. 0 s) | post-stimulus bins | peak | peak time |\n   637→|--------|----------------------------------|--------------------|------|-----------|\n   638→| choice | 0.572 | 0.632 | **0.720** | **+0.30 s** |\n   639→| prior | 0.650 | 0.675 | 0.710 | +0.28 s |\n   640→| wheel_speed | 0.440 | 0.527 | 0.609 | +0.22 s |\n   641→| whisker ME | 0.460 | 0.536 | 0.558 | +0.22 s |\n   642→\n   643→The choice time course is exactly the physiologically expected one: flat at 0.57-0.58 before\n   644→the stimulus (IBL choice is partly predictable from the block prior and the previous trial even\n   645→before stimulus onset), a sharp rise starting ~0.1 s after onset, a peak of 0.72 at +0.30 s -\n   646→the median first-wheel-movement latency - and a slow decay afterwards. A temporal-alignment\n   647→error, a shuffled label or a wrong sign convention would all destroy this structure. The\n   648→whole-trial average of 0.615 is the arithmetic consequence of averaging informative and\n   649→uninformative bins, not a defect.\n   650→\n   651→Two further experiments on the same subset:\n   652→* **Is the 20 ms bin the limit?** Summing the 20 ms bins in groups of 5 (100 ms) raises\n   653→  per-timestep choice from 0.617 to 0.654 and prior from 0.669 to 0.743. The per-bin\n   654→  signal-to-noise of a 20 ms window is the binding constraint, not the conversion. 20 ms was\n   655→  kept because it is what the released reference code uses, what the method paper's Results\n   656→  state (\"2-s trials ... 20-ms bins ... T = 100\"), and what the two time-varying outputs need.\n   657→  (The STAR Methods mention 50 ms for choice/prior but 20 ms for the dynamic behaviours; a\n   658→  single binning has to serve all four outputs here, and only 20 ms is implemented in the\n   659→  released code.)\n   660→* **Pooling bins into one prediction per trial** (`cache/per_trial_choice.py`, majority vote\n   661→  over the post-stimulus bins) gives per-trial balanced accuracy **0.70 for choice** and\n   662→  **0.735 for prior** on 5,174 held-out trials.\n   663→\n   664→### Check 2: Accuracy comparison to the reference papers\n   665→| Variable | Reference value | Reference protocol | Ours | Comparable? |\n   666→|----------|----------------|--------------------|------|-------------|\n   667→| choice | AUC **0.869** (good units) / 0.896 (all units), region `all` | per-**trial** label from the whole N x 100 trial matrix, single-session reduced-rank/L1 model with a per-session hyperparameter sweep | per-timestep balanced acc 0.615; per-trial balanced acc **0.70** (~AUC 0.77-0.80) | partly |\n   668→| prior | R2 0.182 (good units) / 0.270 | per-trial *continuous* Bayes-optimal prior | 3-class block prior, per-trial balanced acc **0.735** (chance 0.333) | no (different target) |\n   669→| wheel speed | R2 ~0.4-0.6 (figures only) | continuous, movement-aligned, 20 ms | 3-class, balanced acc 0.611 (chance 0.333) | no (different target) |\n   670→| whisker ME | R2 ~0.5-0.7 (figures only) | continuous, movement-aligned, 20 ms | 3-class, balanced acc 0.592 (chance 0.333) | no (different target) |\n   671→| choice (data paper) | per-region null-corrected balanced accuracy, mostly +0.02 .. +0.10 over 0.5 | single region, 100 ms window | +0.115 over chance pooled over all bins, +0.22 at the peak bin | ours is higher, as expected when pooling all regions |\n   672→\n   673→The only directly comparable number is choice. The residual gap (0.70 per-trial balanced\n   674→accuracy, ~AUC 0.78, vs the reference's 0.869 AUC with good units) is attributable to the\n   675→decoder rather than the data, and its components are measurable:\n   676→1. The provided decoder sees **one 20 ms bin at a time** (100 PCs + 2 inputs -> logistic),\n   677→   while the reference fits all 100 bins of a trial jointly (N x T, up to 51,600 features).\n   678→   The 100 ms experiment above shows ~+0.04 balanced accuracy from 5x more spikes per row\n   679→   alone.\n   680→2. The reference sweeps learning rate, weight decay and rank per session; the provided decoder\n   681→   uses one fixed configuration and one shared output head across all 441 sessions.\n   682→3. The reference standardises spike counts per time bin before fitting\n   683→   (`standardize_spike_data`); the provided decoder does not, so high-rate neurons dominate\n   684→   the SVD-initialised projection.\n   685→None of these can be changed by the conversion: the neural data, the alignment and the labels\n   686→were all verified against the raw files in Step 10. (An AUC could not be computed directly\n   687→from the provided decoder: `predict()` returns only `softmax(logits).max()`, the confidence of\n   688→the *predicted* class, not a signed class score.)\n   689→\n   690→### Check 3: Train vs validation gap\n   691→| Output | Train | Validation | Ratio |\n   692→|--------|-------|-----------|-------|\n   693→| choice | 0.6384 | 0.6153 | 1.04x |\n   694→| prior | 0.6787 | 0.6604 | 1.03x |\n   695→| wheel_speed | 0.6174 | 0.6110 | 1.01x |\n   696→| whisker ME | 0.5985 | 0.5921 | 1.01x |\n   697→\n   698→All ratios are <=1.04x, far below the 1.5x threshold: no overfitting, no data leakage. (The\n   699→split is per session, 80/20 over trials, so leakage would have to be within a trial, and every\n   700→input and output is derived only from that trial's own window.)\n   701→\n   702→### Debugging steps applied to the weakest output (choice)\n   703→1. **Output values verified against raw data**: `cache/sanity_checks.py` re-derives choice for\n   704→   every retained trial of 4 sessions from the raw trials table - exact match; and on\n   705→   100%-contrast trials a left stimulus yields the \"left\" label in 98.5-100% of trials, which\n   706→   independently confirms the `choice == +1 => left` sign convention.\n   707→2. **Temporal alignment verified**: the population PSTH in `processing_<eid>.png` is flat\n   708→   before 0 and jumps from 6.5 Hz to 11.7 Hz peaking at +0.25 s; the per-timepoint choice\n   709→   accuracy peaks at +0.30 s; the discretised wheel speed steps up at bin 25 of 100.\n   710→3. **Class balance verified**: choice is 0.508/0.492 overall and every session contains both\n   711→   classes (per-session left fraction, 5-95% range: 0.35-0.65).\n   712→4. **Neural filtering verified**: units/probe (891 vs 889) and well-isolated units/probe\n   713→   (108.6 vs 108) reproduce the data paper.\n   714→5. **Processing verified against the reference code**: stage-by-stage comparison in Step 10,\n   715→   Check 3.\n   716→\n   717→### Issues Found and Resolved\n   718→- *38 all-zero-neural warnings* -> minimum of 5 neurons per session and removal of zero-spike\n   719→  trials (Step 10, Check 1). Its parameters:\n\n```python\nparams = {'interval_len': 2, 'binsize': 0.02, 'single_region': False,\n          'align_time': 'stimOn_times', 'time_window': (-.5, 1.5)}\nbeh_names = ['choice', 'reward', 'block', 'wheel-speed', 'whisker-motion-energy']\n```\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `prepare_data(one, eid, bwm_df, params)` | `src/utils/ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | Per-eid entry point: loads all probes' spike sorting, merges probes, loads trials + trial mask, loads continuous behaviours |\n| `load_spiking_data(one, pid, qc=None)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING/CURATION | `SpikeSortingLoader.load_spike_sorting()` + `merge_clusters()`; `qc=None` ⇒ keep **all** clusters, `qc=1` ⇒ keep clusters with `label >= 1` (IBL \"good\"/well-isolated units) |\n| `merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | Concatenates probes of one session into one population, re-indexing `spikes['clusters']`, re-sorting spikes by time |\n| `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2., nan_exclude='default', max_trial_len=10.0, exclude_nochoice=True)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Returns full trials table + boolean mask of usable trials |\n| `list_brain_regions` / `select_brain_regions` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Maps cluster `acronym` → **Beryl** atlas mapping, selects clusters in requested regions (`single_region=False` ⇒ all regions together) |\n| `bin_spiking_data(reg_clu_ids, neural_df, trials_df, **params)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Builds intervals `stimOn + (-0.5, 1.5)`, bins spikes at 20 ms via `bincount2D`, returns `(n_trials, T=100, n_clusters)` |\n| `get_spike_data_per_interval` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Per-interval `bincount2D(times, clusters, xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])`, keeps first `n_bins = ceil(2/0.02) = 100` columns |\n| `load_target_behavior(one, eid, target)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | LOADING | `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` → `wheel-speed` = `abs(velocity)`; `SessionLoader.load_motion_energy(views=['left'])` → `leftCamera.whiskerMotionEnergy` |\n| `bin_behaviors(one, eid, behaviors, trials_df, **params)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING | Per-trial: `choice`, `block` (= `probabilityLeft`), `reward` (`rewardVolume>1`), `contrast`; time-varying behaviours via `get_behavior_per_interval` |\n| `get_behavior_per_interval` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | PROCESSING/CURATION | Slices behaviour samples inside each interval, linearly interpolates onto `np.linspace(t_beg + binsize, t_end, 100)`; marks interval bad if no samples, if it \"starts too late\"/\"ends too early\" (> 1 binsize gap at either edge), or if interval times are NaN |\n| `align_spike_behavior(binned_spikes, binned_behaviors, beh_names, trials_mask)` | `ibl_data_utils.py` | CURATION | Drops trials failing the trials mask *or* missing any behaviour, keeping neural and behaviour trial-aligned |\n| `standardize_spike_data` | `src/utils/data_loader_utils.py` | PROCESSING (model side) | Per-time-bin z-scoring; happens at model-fit time, **not** in the cached dataset |\n| `create_dataset` | `src/utils/dataset_utils.py` | SAVING | Stores binned spikes as `np.ubyte` CSR — i.e. the cached neural data are **raw spike counts**, not rates |\n\n### Notes\n- Answers to the checklist questions: this is **electrophysiology**, so no ΔF/F. Cell quality\n  filtering *is* available (`load_spiking_data(qc=...)` on the IBL `label` metric) — see Step 4\n  for the decision.\n- `load_spiking_data` also calls `spike_loader.raw_electrophysiology(band=\"ap\", stream=True).fs`\n  purely to record the AP sampling frequency in metadata. That streams raw binary from the\n  remote server and is impossible (and pointless) offline — omitted.\n- `SessionLoader` in the installed ibllib 4.0.1 is keyword-only (`SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)`),\n  while the 2025 reference code calls it positionally. Reference helpers are therefore called\n  with an explicitly-constructed `sess_loader`.\n- Example decoder outputs shipped with the reference code\n  (`data/example_decoder_outputs/<eid>/<target>/<region>/metrics/fold_*.npy`) give per-fold\n  metrics under four neuron-selection strategies: `all_ks`, `good_ks`, `thresholded`,\n  `density_based`. Averaged over the 17 example sessions × 5 folds, region `all`:\n\n  | target | all_ks | good_ks | thresholded | density_based |\n  |--------|--------|---------|-------------|---------------|\n  | choice (AUC) | 0.896 | 0.869 | 0.879 | 0.884 |\n  | prior (R²)   | 0.270 | 0.182 | 0.248 | 0.247 |\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\nONE cache, standard IBL ALF layout:\n\n```\n/app/data/one_cache/\n  Brainwidemap/{sessions,datasets}.pqt      # release tables: 480 sessions, 76,563 datasets\n  2022_Q4_IBL_et_al_BWM/…                   # 354 sessions\n  2025_Q3_IBL_et_al_BWM/…                   # 459 sessions, 5,339 datasets (video revisions)\n  .rest/                                    # 6,483 cached Alyx REST responses\n  <lab>/Subjects/<subject>/<date>/<number>/alf/\n      _ibl_trials.table.pqt, _ibl_trials.{goCueTrigger_times,stimOff_times}.npy\n      _ibl_wheel.{position,timestamps}.npy\n      {left,right}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy, _ibl_{left,right}Camera.times.npy\n      probe0N/pykilosort/{spikes.times,spikes.clusters,spikes.depths}.npy\n                        {clusters.channels,clusters.depths,clusters.metrics.pqt}\n                        channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy\n```\n\nOnly the files needed for this analysis are staged (16 per session), not the full 76,563-dataset\nrelease; everything is loaded through `ONE` / `brainbox` loaders, never by reading files directly.\n\nKey variables:\n- `trials` table (20 columns): `stimOn_times`, `goCue_times`, `goCueTrigger_times`,\n  `firstMovement_times`, `response_times`, `feedback_times`, `feedbackType`, `choice`,\n  `contrastLeft`, `contrastRight`, `probabilityLeft`, `rewardVolume`, `intervals_0/1`,\n  `stimOff_times`, `quiescencePeriod`, …\n  - `choice ∈ {-1, 0, +1}`; verified empirically (see Step 4) that `choice == +1` is a **left**\n    report and `choice == -1` a **right** report; `0` = no-go.\n  - `probabilityLeft ∈ {0.2, 0.5, 0.8}`.\n- `wheel`: `SessionLoader.load_wheel()` returns position interpolated to 1 kHz plus Gaussian-smoothed\n  `velocity`/`acceleration`.\n- `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (whisker motion energy) at ~60 Hz; `rightCamera` at ~150 Hz.\n- clusters table after `merge_clusters`: IBL QC metrics (`amp_median`, `noise_cutoff`,\n  `slidingRP_viol`, `label`, `firing_rate`, …) plus Allen `acronym` per cluster.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files / release table)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions (BWM freeze `bwm_release.csv`) | 459 |\n| Probe insertions | 699 (701 probe dirs staged) |\n| Subjects | 139 |\n| Sessions / subject | 1–? (mean 3.3) |\n| Session dirs staged | 461 |\n| Sessions with `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` | 439 |\n| Sessions with `rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` | 422 |\n| Clusters / probe (example sessions) | 898, 1728 (2 probes), 1202 (2 probes) |\n| Good clusters (`label >= 1`) / session | 76, 264, 163 in the 3 probed examples |\n| Trials / session (raw) | 565, 425, 557 in the same examples |\n| Trials / session passing reference mask | 407, 244, 148 |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Mice | 139 | \"We trained 139 mice (94 male and 45 female)\" (data paper) |\n| Probes | 699 | \"we inserted 699 Neuropixels probes\" |\n| Sessions released | 459 | \"a total of 459 sessions, 699 insertions and 621,733 neurons remained\" |\n| Units (all Kilosort) | 621,733 (avg 889/probe) | \"This process produced 621,733 units (including multineuron activity), averaging 889 per probe\" |\n| **Well-isolated neurons** | **75,708 (avg 108/probe)** | \"identified 75,708 well-isolated neurons, averaging 108 per probe\" |\n| Sessions used by method paper | **433** | \"We apply our models to 433 IBL sessions, covering 270 brain regions\" |\n| Brain regions | **270** | same |\n| Trials/session floor | ≥250 (release), ≥400 (analyses) | \"mice performed at least 250 trials\"; \"Only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained\" |\n| Neural data time bin | **20 ms**, T = **100** | \"Recordings are split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps\" |\n| Behaviour sampling | 60 Hz (left cam) | \"wheel speed and whisker motion energy are time-varying signals sampled at 60 Hz\" |\n| Alignment for choice | stimulus onset, −0.5 → +1.5 s | \"For choice, we align trials to the stimulus onset, considering neural activity from 0.5 s before to 1.5 s post-onset\" |\n| Reward rate | ~0.85 (measured on example session) | task performance ≥90% on 100% contrast trials |\n| Block structure | first 90 trials at 0.5; then 0.2/0.8 blocks of 20–100 trials, mean 51 | \"After an initial 90 unbiased trials… empirical mean of 51 trials\" |\n| Contrast set | {0, 6.25, 12.5, 25, 100}% | data paper |\n| Choice decoding (Zhang, region `all`) | AUC 0.90 (all units) / 0.87 (good units) | example decoder outputs |\n| Prior decoding (Zhang, region `all`) | R² 0.27 / 0.18 | example decoder outputs |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Alignment**: `stimOn_times`, window `(-0.5, +1.5) s` ⇒ 2 s interval.\n- **Binning**: 20 ms non-overlapping ⇒ T = 100. Spike bin `k` spans\n  `[stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02k, stimOn − 0.5 + 0.02(k+1))` (this is exactly what `bincount2D`\n  with `xlim=[t_beg,t_end]` does, and the reference then truncates to the first 100 columns).\n- **Continuous behaviour**: linearly interpolated onto `t_beg + 0.02·(k+1)`, i.e. the **right edge**\n  of each spike bin.\n- The method paper also describes 50-ms bins in a −0.5/+1.5 s window for choice and a\n  movement-aligned 0/+1 s window for the dynamic behaviours; the *released caching code*\n  (which is what is reproducible and which produces one dataset carrying all four behaviours)\n  uses the stimulus-onset 2 s / 20 ms / T=100 configuration, which is also the configuration\n  stated in the paper's Results (\"2-s trials… 20-ms bins… T = 100\"). The Decoder Task here\n  fixes stimulus-onset alignment, so that configuration is used.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (data paper \"Neurons and brain regions\"):\n- Exclude units failing any of the three RIGOR single-unit metrics: amplitude > 50 µV,\n  noise cut-off < 20 µV, refractory-period violation. Units passing all three are\n  \"well-isolated neurons\" (75,708 of 621,733). In the IBL cluster table this is exactly\n  `label >= 1` (`label` is the fraction of the three metrics passed: 0, 1/3, 2/3, 1).\n- Analyses further restricted to **grey-matter** regions in the Allen CCF, with ≥5 neurons\n  per session and recorded in ≥2 sessions.\n\n**Trial curation rules** (data paper \"Trials\", implemented in `load_trials_and_mask`):\n- Exclude a trial if any of `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`,\n  `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType` is NaN.\n- Exclude if reaction time (`firstMovement_times − stimOn_times`) ∉ [0.08, 2.00] s.\n- Reference code adds `max_trial_len=10.0` (`feedback_times − goCue_times ≤ 10 s`) and\n  `exclude_nochoice=True` (`choice != 0`).\n\n### Decoders Trained (reference)\n| Decoded variable | Metric | Reference value (region `all`) |\n|------------------|--------|-------------------------------|\n| choice | AUC | 0.896 (all units) / 0.869 (good units) |\n| prior | R² | 0.270 / 0.182 |\n| wheel speed | R² | reported only in figures (single-trial R², ≈0.4–0.6) |\n| whisker motion energy | R² | reported only in figures (≈0.5–0.7) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Neuron quality filter | `load_spiking_data(qc=None)` ⇒ **all** Kilosort clusters | `label>=1` selects ~8–15% of clusters (76/898, 264/1728, 163/1202) | Method paper: \"bin spike counts using **all neurons**, sorted by Kilosort 2.5\"; data paper: analyses restricted to the **75,708 well-isolated neurons** | **Use well-isolated neurons (`label >= 1`)**. Rationale below. |\n| Brain-region set | `list_brain_regions` keeps every Beryl acronym incl. `root`/`void`; `MultiRegionDataModule` drops `root`/`void` | 15–20% of good clusters map to `root`/`void` | Data paper restricts to Allen **grey matter** | Drop `root`/`void` (non-grey-matter / unassigned), keep everything else. Gives a Beryl region list comparable to the paper's 270 regions. |\n| Time bin | code: 20 ms, T=100 | — | Results: 20 ms, T=100; STAR Methods \"data processing\": 50 ms for choice/prior, 20 ms for dynamic behaviours | Use **20 ms, T = 100**: it matches the released code and the Results text, and the Decoder Task requires one common binning for all four outputs. |\n| Alignment | code: `stimOn_times` | — | STAR Methods uses `firstMovement_times` for the dynamic behaviours | Use **`stimOn_times`** — mandated by the Decoder Task and by the released caching code. |\n| `allow_nans` for behaviours | reference passes `allow_nans=True` and later imputes NaNs with the trial mean inside the data loader | motion energy occasionally has NaN runs | — | Drop trials whose wheel-speed or whisker-ME window contains NaN. The target format forbids NaN, and imputing a value then discretising it would fabricate labels. |\n| Sessions | freeze file lists 459 eids | 461 session dirs staged | Method paper used **433** sessions | Start from the 459-eid freeze, drop sessions with no usable whisker video, no surviving neurons, or <2 usable trials; expect to land near 433. |\n| `choice` sign | code stores raw `choice` | on 100%-contrast trials: stim-left ⇒ `choice=+1` in 71/72 trials, stim-right ⇒ `choice=−1` in 61/63 | mice report the stimulus side | `choice == +1` ⇒ **left** report ⇒ label 0; `choice == −1` ⇒ **right** report ⇒ label 1. |\n\n**Why well-isolated neurons rather than \"all neurons\":**\n1. The data paper — the source of these data — explicitly defines its analysis population as the\n   75,708 well-isolated neurons and excludes the rest as multi-unit activity.\n2. The reference code exposes exactly this switch (`qc=1` ⇒ `label >= 1`) and its `merge_probes`\n   docstring is written against `brainwidemap.load_good_units`.\n3. Feasibility: all 621,733 units at T=100 × ~300 usable trials × 459 sessions would be ≈120 GB of\n   float32 — not a storable/trainable dataset. Well-isolated units give ≈8 GB.\n4. Cost is small and quantified: the reference's own example outputs show choice AUC 0.896 with\n   all units vs 0.869 with good units (−3%).\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` (all probes, merged) | `neural[s][k]` (n_neurons, 100) float32 | spike counts in 20 ms bins over `stimOn + [-0.5, 1.5)` | `merge_probes`, `bin_spiking_data`/`bincount2D` | raw counts, no rate conversion, no z-scoring (reference z-scores inside the model's data loader, not in the cached data) |\n| bin right-edge time relative to `stimOn_times` | `input[s][k][0, :]` | `-0.5 + 0.02·(k+1)` ∈ [−0.48, 1.50] | `get_behavior_per_interval`'s `x_interp` grid | \"time since stimulus onset\", continuous, time-varying |\n| index of the trial within its `probabilityLeft` block | `input[s][k][1, :]` | 0-based count from block start, computed on the **unfiltered** trials table, broadcast over T | — (new; required by Decoder Task) | \"trial number in block\", continuous, per-trial → broadcast to time-varying |\n| `trials.choice` | `output[s][k][0, :]` | `+1 → 0` (left), `−1 → 1` (right); broadcast over T | `bin_behaviors` (`choice`) | binary, per-trial |\n| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[s][k][1, :]` | `0.2→0, 0.5→1, 0.8→2`; broadcast over T | `bin_behaviors` (`block`) | 3-way, per-trial |\n| `SessionLoader.wheel.velocity` | `output[s][k][2, :]` | `abs(velocity)` → interp to bin edges → per-session tertiles → {0,1,2} | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | time-varying |\n| `leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy` (fallback `rightCamera`) | `output[s][k][3, :]` | interp to bin edges → per-session tertiles → {0,1,2} | `load_target_behavior('left-whisker-motion-energy')` | time-varying |\n| `clusters.acronym` → Beryl | `brain_region_idx[s]`, `brain_regions` | `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., 'Beryl')` | `list_brain_regions` | `root`/`void` dropped |\n| `bwm_release.csv` `subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique sorted list | — | |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Alignment / window / binning**: `stimOn_times`, `(-0.5, +1.5) s`, 20 ms ⇒ T = 100.\n   Exactly the reference `params`; also required by the Decoder Task.\n2. **Neuron curation**: IBL `label >= 1` (all three RIGOR metrics passed) **and** Beryl region\n   ∉ {`root`, `void`}. Sessions are merged across probes first (`merge_probes`), as the reference\n   and the data paper both do (\"neurons in the same session and region were combined across probes\").\n   The data paper's extra region criteria (≥5 neurons/session, region seen in ≥2 sessions) are\n   *not* applied: they exist to make region-wise statistical maps comparable, whereas here every\n   session is decoded from its whole population, and applying them would delete neurons that\n   carry signal for a session-level decoder.\n3. **Trial curation**: `load_trials_and_mask(min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2.0, nan_exclude='default',\n   max_trial_len=10.0, exclude_nochoice=True)` — the reference call verbatim — plus the\n   reference's behaviour-coverage checks (`get_behavior_per_interval`), plus rejection of\n   trials with NaN in either continuous behaviour.\n4. **Neural values**: raw spike counts stored as float32 (matching the reference's `ubyte` CSR\n   cache). Standardisation is a model-side step in the reference and the provided decoder does\n   its own SVD projection.\n5. **Discretisation of continuous outputs**: per-session tertiles (33.3 / 66.7 percentiles) of the\n   binned values pooled over all retained trials of that session, giving classes\n   `low`/`medium`/`high`. Per-session rather than global because whisker motion energy is in\n   arbitrary units that depend on camera, illumination and ROI size and is therefore not\n   comparable across sessions; tertiles because the Decoder Task asks for 3 bins and balanced\n   accuracy is the evaluation metric, so equal-frequency bins are the natural choice.\n6. **Everything time-varying**: `input` and `output` are both `(d, 100)` per trial; per-trial\n   quantities are broadcast across time, as the format instructions prefer.\n7. **Session exclusion**: sessions from the 459-eid BWM freeze; dropped if no whisker motion\n   energy is available, if no neuron survives curation, or if fewer than 2 trials survive.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] #sessions ≈ 433 (method paper), #subjects ≤ 139, Beryl regions ≈ 270\n- [ ] mean good neurons/session ≈ 75,708 / 459 ≈ 165 before grey-matter filtering\n- [ ] T == 100 for every trial; `input[0]` range == [−0.48, 1.50]\n- [ ] choice class balance near 50/50; prior classes ≈ {0.2: 0.42, 0.5: 0.16, 0.8: 0.42}\n- [ ] wheel-speed and whisker-ME class fractions ≈ 1/3 each by construction\n- [ ] spike counts non-negative integers; mean firing rate per neuron in a plausible 0.1–50 Hz range\n- [ ] independent re-derivation of one trial's spike counts, input, and each output from\n      freshly loaded ONE objects (`np.allclose`) — Step 10\n- [ ] trial-in-block resets exactly at `probabilityLeft` changes and the first block has 90 trials\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan. Structure:\n\n| Function | Role | Reference counterpart |\n|----------|------|-----------------------|\n| `get_one()` | per-worker offline ONE client (`tables_dir=.../Brainwidemap`) | `0_data_caching.py` header |\n| `load_session_spikes` | `SpikeSortingLoader.load_spike_sorting` + `merge_clusters` per probe, then `merge_probes` | `prepare_data` / `load_spiking_data` / `merge_probes` |\n| `select_neurons` | `label >= 1` AND Beryl acronym ∉ {root, void} | `load_spiking_data(qc=1)` + `list_brain_regions`/`select_brain_regions` |\n| `load_trials` | `load_trials_and_mask(max_trial_len=10.0)` (reference function, imported verbatim) | identical |\n| `load_continuous_behaviors` | `SessionLoader.load_wheel` → abs(velocity); `load_motion_energy(['left'])` with right-camera fallback | `load_target_behavior`, `bin_behaviors` |\n| `bin_spikes` | vectorised equivalent of `bincount2D(..., xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg,t_end])[:, :100]` | `get_spike_data_per_interval` |\n| `interpolate_behavior` | same slicing (`searchsorted` right/left), same target grid `linspace(t_beg+bin, t_end, 100)`, same four skip rules | `get_behavior_per_interval` |\n| `discretize_tertiles` | per-session 33.3/66.7 percentile split | new (categorical-output requirement) |\n| `trial_number_in_block` | 0-based index within each constant-`probabilityLeft` run, on the unfiltered table | new (Decoder Task input) |\n\nCode inefficiencies identified in the reference:\n- `get_spike_data_per_interval` spawns a multiprocessing pool **per session** and calls\n  `bincount2D` once **per trial**, which dominates its runtime.\n- `get_behavior_per_interval` likewise pools per behaviour per session.\n- `prepare_data` streams the raw AP band from the server only to read its sampling rate.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Spike binning replaced by a single `np.searchsorted` for all trial edges plus one\n  `np.bincount` on `cluster*T + bin` per trial: 0.01 s/session instead of pool startup +\n  ~400 `bincount2D` calls.\n- Parallelism moved up one level: one worker process per **session** (default 24), so the\n  pools are created once for the whole run instead of ~2000 times.\n- The raw-ephys sampling-rate query is dropped (metadata only, needs the network).\n- Spikes are binned only for trials that survive curation.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n(→ `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`), They were traced to (a) one session with a\nsingle surviving neuron and one with ten, and (b) short dropouts in three otherwise normal\nsessions (e.g. session 392: three consecutive trials with no spikes from 108 neurons).\nBoth causes were fixed rather than tolerated:\n* `MIN_NEURONS_PER_SESSION = 5` - the data paper's \"at least five well-isolated neurons per\n  session\" criterion applied at the session level, because here the decoded population is the\n  whole session. Removes 3 sessions.\n* trials in which the entire population records zero spikes over the whole 2 s window are\n  dropped as recording dropouts (16 trials, 0.009%).\nThe re-run produced zero warnings.\n\n### Check 2: Independent sanity checks (`cache/sanity_checks.py`)\nEvery quantity is re-derived from the original data loaded through ONE/brainbox, using\ndifferent code from `convert_data.py`, and compared with `np.allclose`. Run on 4 randomly\nchosen sessions: **96 checks, 96 passed, 0 failed.**\n\n| Check | How it is made independent | Result |\n|-------|---------------------------|--------|\n| neuron count and region labels | clusters re-loaded per probe, `label`/Beryl re-derived | exact match |\n| spike counts, 6 random (trial, neuron) pairs per session | `np.histogram` with explicit 101 bin edges instead of `np.bincount` on flattened indices | `allclose` on all 100 bins |\n| whole-trial population spike totals, 3 trials per session | boolean mask on raw spike times | exact |\n| input 0 (time from stim onset) | grid rebuilt from constants | `allclose` |\n| input 1 (trial in block) | recomputed with a plain python loop over the raw trials table | `allclose` |\n| first unbiased block is 90 trials | from the raw `probabilityLeft` | 90 in every session |\n| output 0 (choice) | `np.where(choice == 1, 0, 1)` from the raw table | `allclose` |\n| output 1 (prior) | `np.select` on raw `probabilityLeft` | `allclose` |\n| output 2 (wheel speed class) | `SessionLoader` reloaded, `np.interp` (a different interpolator) + independent tertiles | 99.99-100.0% of bins identical; thresholds `allclose` |\n| output 3 (whisker ME class) | same, on the camera recorded in metadata | 99.6-99.8% of bins identical; thresholds `allclose` |\n| behavioural plausibility | performance on 100%-contrast trials, and left-stimulus -> left-choice rate | 0.98-1.00 and 0.99-1.00 |\n\nThe <0.4% of whisker-ME bins that differ are values sitting exactly on a tertile boundary: the\nchecker uses `np.interp`, which clamps at the ends of the sample range, whereas the converter\nuses `scipy.interp1d(fill_value='extrapolate')` restricted to in-interval samples, exactly as\n`get_behavior_per_interval` does. The difference is at most one 60 Hz sample at the right edge\nof a trial and never moves a threshold by more than 0.1%.\n\n### Check 3: Reference code comparison\n| Stage | Reference (`ibl_data_utils.py` / `0_data_caching.py`) | `convert_data.py` | Same? |\n|-------|------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|-------|\n| (a) loading | `SpikeSortingLoader.load_spike_sorting` + `merge_clusters(compute_metrics=False)` per pid from `one.eid2pid`, then `merge_probes` | identical calls; pids taken from `bwm_release.csv` instead of `eid2pid` (same mapping, no REST round-trip); the `raw_electrophysiology(...).fs` metadata query is skipped | yes, apart from the omitted metadata field |\n| (b) neuron filtering | `qc=None` => all clusters; `list_brain_regions` keeps every Beryl acronym | `label >= 1` (= reference's `qc=1`) and Beryl not in {root, void}; >=5 neurons per session | **deliberate difference**, justified in Step 4 |\n| (b) trial filtering | `load_trials_and_mask(one, eid, max_trial_len=10.0)` then `align_spike_behavior` | the **same reference function**, imported and called with the same arguments; behaviour-missing trials removed exactly as `align_spike_behavior` does | yes |\n| (c) alignment | `intervals = stimOn + (-0.5, 1.5)` | identical | yes |\n| (d) binning | `bincount2D(t, c, xbin=0.02, xlim=[t_beg, t_end])[:, :100]`, spikes with `t_beg <= t < t_end` | `floor((t - t_beg)/0.02)` + `np.bincount`, spikes selected by `searchsorted` on the same half-open interval - algebraically the same operation, ~1000x faster | yes (verified against `np.histogram` in Check 2) |\n| (d) behaviour resampling | samples strictly inside the interval, `interp1d(..., fill_value='extrapolate')` onto `linspace(t_beg+bin, t_end, 100)`, four skip rules | identical, same `searchsorted` sides, same grid, same skip rules; `allow_nans` set to False instead of True | yes, except NaN handling (documented) |\n| (e) inputs | reference has no decoder-input concept (its models take only spikes) | time-from-onset grid = the reference's behaviour interpolation grid; trial-in-block is new, required by the Decoder Task | n/a |\n| (f) outputs | `choice`, `block` = `probabilityLeft`, `wheel-speed` = `abs(velocity)`, `whisker-motion-energy` = left (else right) camera ROI motion energy | the same four variables from the same fields, mapped to the class codes required by the Decoder Task and (for the two continuous ones) tertile-discretised | yes, plus the required discretisation |\n| storage | `ubyte` CSR of raw spike counts, no normalisation | float32 raw spike counts, no normalisation | yes |\n\nDifferences and their reasons, in full:\n1. **Well-isolated units only.** The data paper defines its analysis population this way; the\n   reference code exposes the switch (`qc=1`); all units would be ~120 GB. Measured cost from\n   the reference's own example outputs: choice AUC 0.896 -> 0.869.\n2. **Grey matter only (`root`/`void` dropped).** Data paper: \"restricted to regions that were\n   designated grey matter\". `root`/`void` are not brain regions and would pollute\n   `brain_regions`.\n3. **>=5 neurons per session.** The data paper's per-region floor applied to the decoded\n   population; also removes the degenerate 1-neuron session.\n4. **NaN behaviour trials dropped** instead of NaN-imputed at model-fit time: the target format\n   forbids NaN and a categorical label cannot be imputed without inventing a class.\n5. **Zero-spike trials dropped** (16 trials): recording dropouts.\n6. **No AP sampling-rate metadata**: requires streaming raw data from the server.\n7. **Tertile discretisation** of the two continuous behaviours: required by the\n   categorical-output specification.\n\n### Check 4: Key statistics comparison\nSee the table in Step 9. Every statistic the papers report is reproduced: units/probe 891 vs\n889, well-isolated units/probe 108.6 vs 108, sessions 441 vs 433-459, subjects 136 vs 139,\nBeryl regions 263 vs 270, T = 100, 20 ms bins, choice ~50/50, performance on 100% contrast\n> 0.9.\n\nTwo small deficits were investigated:\n* **263 vs 270 regions.** Expected: Zhang et al. counted regions over all Kilosort units in 433\n  sessions, while we count only well-isolated grey-matter units, so rare regions with no\n  well-isolated unit drop out. 240 of our regions appear in >=2 sessions, the data paper's own\n  reportability criterion.\n* **441 vs 433 sessions.** We keep slightly more because Zhang et al. additionally require\n  pupil traces in some analyses and their caching script silently skips any session that\n  raises. Our 18 exclusions are each explicitly enumerated with a reason.\n\n### Check 5: Edge cases (`cache/edge_cases.py`)\n25 structural/edge-case assertions over all 441 sessions, all passing: every session has >=2\ntrials (min 125) and both choice classes and >=2 prior classes; T == 100 everywhere; neuron\ncount constant within a session; `brain_region_idx` lengths and all index ranges valid; no\n`root`/`void` in `brain_regions`; spike counts non-negative integers with no all-zero trial at\nsession edges; input 0 equals the bin-edge grid on the first and last trial of every session;\ninput 1 and outputs 0-1 constant within a trial; trial-in-block in [0, 93]; output ranges\n[0,1] and [0,2]; `session_info` aligned with the session list; `trial_idx` strictly\nincreasing; metadata `off_start`/`off_end`/`time_bin_size` correct.\n\nRobustness handled in the conversion code: sessions with one or two probes; sessions with only\na right camera (7 of 441); sessions with no camera at all (14, skipped); `probabilityLeft`\nvalues outside {0.2, 0.5, 0.8} (hard error, never triggered); trials whose 2 s window is not\ncovered by the behavioural traces (86 dropped); sessions where every trial fails (1, skipped);\ndegenerate tertiles (fallback to whatever distinct quantiles exist).\n\n### Iterations\n1. **Iteration 1** - first full run: 444 sessions, 38 \"all neural data is zero\" warnings. Fixed\n   with the >=5-neuron and zero-spike-trial rules; re-ran the conversion, the verification and\n   all checks. Result: 441 sessions, 0 warnings, 0 failed checks.\n2. **Iteration 2** - added `trial_idx` to `session_info` so the conversion can be audited\n   against the raw trials table; re-ran the full conversion and every check above.\n\n---\n\n## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples 2>&1 | tee /app/train_decoder_full_out.txt`\n(441 sessions, 187,918 trials, 18.79 M timepoints; L4 GPU, ~25 min)\n\n### Training Progress\n- Loss decreasing: **Yes**, monotonically - 1.947 (epoch 1) -> 1.300 (20) -> 0.967 (50) ->\n  0.786 (100) -> 0.752 (150) -> 0.7418 (200). Test loss 0.7676.\n\n### Decoder Results (Full)\n| Output | Chance (1/n) | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Val / chance | Notes |\n|--------|--------------|-----------------------|-------------------------|--------------|-------|\n| choice | 0.5000 | 0.6384 | **0.6153** | 1.23x | per-timestep; rises to 0.72 at +0.3 s (Step 12) |\n| prior | 0.3333 | 0.6787 | **0.6604** | 1.98x | |\n| wheel_speed | 0.3333 | 0.6174 | **0.6110** | 1.83x | |\n| whisker_motion_energy | 0.3333 | 0.5985 | **0.5921** | 1.78x | |\n\n`sample_trials.png` and `predictions.png` were written. The sample-trial figure confirms the\nintended structure: the time input ramps linearly from -0.48 to 1.50 s, trial-in-block and\nchoice/prior are flat within a trial, and both discretised behaviours step up from class 0 to\nclass 2 around bin 25, i.e. exactly at stimulus onset.\n\n---\n\n## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Check 1: Accuracy vs chance\n| Variable | Chance | Validation | Ratio | Verdict |\n|----------|--------|-----------|-------|---------|\n| choice | 0.500 | 0.615 | 1.23x | below 1.5x - investigated in depth below |\n| prior | 0.333 | 0.660 | 1.98x | fine |\n| wheel_speed | 0.333 | 0.611 | 1.83x | fine |\n| whisker_motion_energy | 0.333 | 0.592 | 1.78x | fine |\n\nNothing is at or below chance. For a **binary** variable, 1.5x chance means 0.75 balanced\naccuracy at **every one of the 100 time bins**, including the 25 bins before the stimulus even\nappears - an impossible bar for a per-timestep decoder, so the ratio was checked against the\ntime course rather than taken at face value.\n\n`cache/timepoint_analysis.py` retrains the provided decoder on a 60-session subset and scores\neach of the 100 bins separately on held-out trials:\n\n| Output | pre-stimulus bins (-0.48 .. 0 s) | post-stimulus bins | peak | peak time |\n|--------|----------------------------------|--------------------|------|-----------|\n| choice | 0.572 | 0.632 | **0.720** | **+0.30 s** |\n| prior | 0.650 | 0.675 | 0.710 | +0.28 s |\n| wheel_speed | 0.440 | 0.527 | 0.609 | +0.22 s |\n| whisker ME | 0.460 | 0.536 | 0.558 | +0.22 s |\n\nThe choice time course is exactly the physiologically expected one: flat at 0.57-0.58 before\nthe stimulus (IBL choice is partly predictable from the block prior and the previous trial even\nbefore stimulus onset), a sharp rise starting ~0.1 s after onset, a peak of 0.72 at +0.30 s -\nthe median first-wheel-movement latency - and a slow decay afterwards. A temporal-alignment\nerror, a shuffled label or a wrong sign convention would all destroy this structure. The\nwhole-trial average of 0.615 is the arithmetic consequence of averaging informative and\nuninformative bins, not a defect.\n\nTwo further experiments on the same subset:\n* **Is the 20 ms bin the limit?** Summing the 20 ms bins in groups of 5 (100 ms) raises\n  per-timestep choice from 0.617 to 0.654 and prior from 0.669 to 0.743. The per-bin\n  signal-to-noise of a 20 ms window is the binding constraint, not the conversion. 20 ms was\n  kept because it is what the released reference code uses, what the method paper's Results\n  state (\"2-s trials ... 20-ms bins ... T = 100\"), and what the two time-varying outputs need.\n  (The STAR Methods mention 50 ms for choice/prior but 20 ms for the dynamic behaviours; a\n  single binning has to serve all four outputs here, and only 20 ms is implemented in the\n  released code.)\n* **Pooling bins into one prediction per trial** (`cache/per_trial_choice.py`, majority vote\n  over the post-stimulus bins) gives per-trial balanced accuracy **0.70 for choice** and\n  **0.735 for prior** on 5,174 held-out trials.\n\n### Check 2: Accuracy comparison to the reference papers\n| Variable | Reference value | Reference protocol | Ours | Comparable? |\n|----------|----------------|--------------------|------|-------------|\n| choice | AUC **0.869** (good units) / 0.896 (all units), region `all` | per-**trial** label from the whole N x 100 trial matrix, single-session reduced-rank/L1 model with a per-session hyperparameter sweep | per-timestep balanced acc 0.615; per-trial balanced acc **0.70** (~AUC 0.77-0.80) | partly |\n| prior | R2 0.182 (good units) / 0.270 | per-trial *continuous* Bayes-optimal prior | 3-class block prior, per-trial balanced acc **0.735** (chance 0.333) | no (different target) |\n| wheel speed | R2 ~0.4-0.6 (figures only) | continuous, movement-aligned, 20 ms | 3-class, balanced acc 0.611 (chance 0.333) | no (different target) |\n| whisker ME | R2 ~0.5-0.7 (figures only) | continuous, movement-aligned, 20 ms | 3-class, balanced acc 0.592 (chance 0.333) | no (different target) |\n| choice (data paper) | per-region null-corrected balanced accuracy, mostly +0.02 .. +0.10 over 0.5 | single region, 100 ms window | +0.115 over chance pooled over all bins, +0.22 at the peak bin | ours is higher, as expected when pooling all regions |\n\nThe only directly comparable number is choice. The residual gap (0.70 per-trial balanced\naccuracy, ~AUC 0.78, vs the reference's 0.869 AUC with good units) is attributable to the\ndecoder rather than the data, and its components are measurable:\n1. The provided decoder sees **one 20 ms bin at a time** (100 PCs + 2 inputs -> logistic),\n   while the reference fits all 100 bins of a trial jointly (N x T, up to 51,600 features).\n   The 100 ms experiment above shows ~+0.04 balanced accuracy from 5x more spikes per row\n   alone.\n2. The reference sweeps learning rate, weight decay and rank per session; the provided decoder\n   uses one fixed configuration and one shared output head across all 441 sessions.\n3. The reference standardises spike counts per time bin before fitting\n   (`standardize_spike_data`); the provided decoder does not, so high-rate neurons dominate\n   the SVD-initialised projection.\nNone of these can be changed by the conversion: the neural data, the alignment and the labels\nwere all verified against the raw files in Step 10. (An AUC could not be computed directly\nfrom the provided decoder: `predict()` returns only `softmax(logits).max()`, the confidence of\nthe *predicted* class, not a signed class score.)\n\n### Check 3: Train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Validation | Ratio |\n|--------|-------|-----------|-------|\n| choice | 0.6384 | 0.6153 | 1.04x |\n| prior | 0.6787 | 0.6604 | 1.03x |\n| wheel_speed | 0.6174 | 0.6110 | 1.01x |\n| whisker ME | 0.5985 | 0.5921 | 1.01x |\n\nAll ratios are <=1.04x, far below the 1.5x threshold: no overfitting, no data leakage. (The\nsplit is per session, 80/20 over trials, so leakage would have to be within a trial, and every\ninput and output is derived only from that trial's own window.)\n\n### Debugging steps applied to the weakest output (choice)\n1. **Output values verified against raw data**: `cache/sanity_checks.py` re-derives choice for\n   every retained trial of 4 sessions from the raw trials table - exact match; and on\n   100%-contrast trials a left stimulus yields the \"left\" label in 98.5-100% of trials, which\n   independently confirms the `choice == +1 => left` sign convention.\n2. **Temporal alignment verified**: the population PSTH in `processing_<eid>.png` is flat\n   before 0 and jumps from 6.5 Hz to 11.7 Hz peaking at +0.25 s; the per-timepoint choice\n   accuracy peaks at +0.30 s; the discretised wheel speed steps up at bin 25 of 100.\n3. **Class balance verified**: choice is 0.508/0.492 overall and every session contains both\n   classes (per-session left fraction, 5-95% range: 0.35-0.65).\n4. **Neural filtering verified**: units/probe (891 vs 889) and well-isolated units/probe\n   (108.6 vs 108) reproduce the data paper.\n5. **Processing verified against the reference code**: stage-by-stage comparison in Step 10,\n   Check 3.\n\n### Issues Found and Resolved\n- *38 all-zero-neural warnings* -> minimum of 5 neurons per session and removal of zero-spike\n  trials (Step 10, Check 1). The metadata that resolution needs is cached under one_cache/.rest, so a run\n    83→    does not have to reach Alyx.\n    84→\n    85→    datadir: directory holding the one_cache/ folder.\n    86→    Sets the globals `one` and `subset_csv`; returns nothing.\n    87→    \"\"\"\n    88→    global one\n    89→    global subset_csv\n    90→    cache = os.path.join(datadir, ONE_CACHE_DIRNAME)\n    91→    tables = os.path.join(cache, RELEASE)\n    92→    client = dict(base_url=BASE_URL, silent=True, cache_dir=cache, tables_dir=tables,\n    93→                  mode='remote')\n    94→    try:\n    95→        # No password: passing one forces re-authentication over the network, while the\n    96→        # token the fetch cached needs no connection.\n    97→        one = ONE(**client)\n    98→    except Exception:\n    99→        one = ONE(password=PASSWORD, **client)\n   100→    one.alyx.default_expiry = REST_EXPIRY\n   101→    # One.load_cache reads the parquet tables where they are; the OneAlyx override would\n   102→    # ask Alyx for a newer copy first.
